AMD (AMD-US)  
財報 FIRST TAKE — AI 機櫃系統躍升營運支柱，化解 PC 逆風，2027 全年營收預估跳增逾 80%

個股基本資料：

 評論及分析 (Overall Summary) 
超微（AMD）3Q26 營收、調整後 EPS 雙雙超越市場共識，主要動能來自
資料中心業務年增近一倍的拉貨力道。雖然 PC 端受記憶體等零組件漲價壓
抑，且帳面認列了 11 億美元非現金權證費用，但 市場疑慮隨即被 4Q26 與
2027 年的強勁展望打消， 不僅 4Q26 營運顯著優於預期，更預告 2027 年
營收將迎來超過 80%  的高速成長 ，激勵盤後股價上漲 。 隨著資料中心 AI 營
收即將在 4Q26 正式超越傳統伺服器 CPU，加上 Helios 整櫃系統 進入實
際出貨期，AMD 正確立以大型 AI 系統為獲利主力的營運格局。後續可留
意 Helios 與 Venice 供不應求下的產能開出速度 ，以及資料中心營收能否
完全填補 PC 衰退缺口 。 
公司財報重點數據： 
 營收：133.8 億美元 （年增 45%、季增 16%），優於市場預期的 130.6 
億美元（幅度 +2.45%）。 
 Non-GAAP 每股盈餘 （EPS）：2.09 美元 （年增 74%），優於市場預期
的 1.94 美元（幅度 +7.73%） ；GAAP EPS 為 1.46 美元（含 11 億
美元認股權證非現金費用）。 
 Non-GAAP 毛利率： 55.9%～56.0%（年增 190 個基點），符合市場
預期的 56.0%。 
 Non-GAAP 營業利益率： 29.3%（年增逾 5 個百分點），優於市場預
期的 28.0%；營業利益達 39 億美元（年增 75%） 。 
公司業績表現： 
 資料中心營收年增近一倍，高階處理器與加速器推升獲利率： 營收達
85.5 億美元 （年增 97%、季增 27%），佔總營收 64%，營業利益大增
168% 至 29 億美元 。 成長主因是雲端客戶對最新 EPYC 伺服器 CPU 
與 Instinct AI 加速器的需求持續大增，規模效益顯著推升獲利。 
 客戶端運算受零組件成本上漲抑制，PC 出貨動能略顯平淡： 營收為
29.5 億美元（年增 7%、季減 4%） ，略低於市場預期，主因是記憶體
等零組件報價墊高，壓縮了下游品牌廠的出貨節奏與拉貨意願。 
 遊戲主機世代進入尾聲，業務呈現結構性衰退：營收降至 7.73 億美元
（年減 40%、季持平），表現雖略高於市場的悲觀預估，但主要受家用
遊戲主機進入產品成熟期後段影響，拉貨力道大幅走緩。 
複委託報告 November 4, 2026

2
 嵌入式庫存調整告一段落，營業利益率重回 41% 高檔： 營收達 11.1
億美元 （年增 29%、季增 14%），優於預期且連續四季成長加速，顯示
工控與網通市場拉貨逐步回到正軌。
公司展望與產業前景：
 4Q26、2027 年成長動能強勁，AI 營收即將正式超車 CPU： 4Q26 營
收指引中位數達 170 億美元 （年增 66%、季增 27%），明顯優於市場
預估。其中，資料中心預期季增約 50%，且 AI 加速器出貨金額將首度
超越伺服器 CPU；2027 年在 Helios 整櫃系統與新一代高核心數
CPU（EPYC Venice）翻倍出貨帶動下，管理層預期全年營收將成長逾
80%，資料中心佔比將突破八成。
 PC 市場 2027 年面臨衰退風險，整櫃出貨毛利率短期略微收斂： 管理
層提醒 2027 年全球 PC 出貨可能出現 雙位數下滑，公司將重心移往
高階商務與桌上型市場以降低衝擊。此外，4Q26 毛利率受 Helios 整
櫃系統與硬體機架出貨佔比提升影響，預期降至 55.0% 左右， 後續記

憶體成本變動仍需留意。
電話會議 Q&A 重點摘要：
 PC 產能可即時轉移至 AI 產線，化解產能閒置隱憂： 針對法人擔心
2027 年 PC 下滑導致晶圓與封裝產能閒置，執行長蘇姿丰明確表示，
PC 與資料中心的供應鏈高度互通， 公司能即時把過剩產能挪給供不應
求的 AI 與伺服器晶片，大幅降低庫存與成本風險 。
 與大客戶分攤記憶體成本，放緩 2027 費用成長步調：面對 HBM 記
憶體漲價，管理層表示已與雲端大客戶簽訂長期分攤協議，確保毛利率
維持在 55% 左右；財務長更明確承諾， 2027 年營業費用成長率將壓
在營收成長率的一半以下，確立獲利增幅將超越營收增幅。
 釐清認股權證轉換條件，短期流通股數不受影響：針對 Q3 認列的 11
億美元權證費用，財務長指出第四季股數維持約 16.7 億股不變 ，該權
證需待客戶在 2027 年達到實質採購里程碑才會逐步轉成實質股數 ， 每
季帳面損益純屬會計調整，不影響實質現金流與 Non-GAAP EPS。
公司簡介：
超微半導體 (AMD-US) 為全球知名高效能運算與半導體設計大廠。主要業

務分為資料中心（EPYC 伺服器處理器、Instinct 系列 AI 加速器） 、客戶端
運算 （Ryzen 處理器）、遊戲晶片 （Radeon 顯卡與遊戲機客製晶片） 以及嵌
入式解決方案。AMD 是業界少數同時擁有頂級 CPU 與高效能 GPU 研發
實力的晶片供應商，在大型雲端運算與 AI 基礎設施領域佔有重要地位。
競爭對手與公司自身價值：

### Page 3

3 
在資料中心 AI 加速器市場，AMD 透過結合 CPU 與 GPU 的 Helios 整
櫃系統切入輝達（Nvidia）主導的市場；在伺服器 CPU 領域，則持續以高
核心數與能效優勢搶奪英特爾（Intel）市佔。AMD 的核心優勢在於能提供
性價比更高的全套運算架構。隨著 2027 年資料中心訂單展望明確且費用控
制嚴格，營運槓桿有望帶動獲利放大，具備中長線投資與分批布局價值。

海外產業訊息評論 
全球生成式 AI 模型開發商 Anthropic(ANT US)上市前瞻 
企業需求帶動營收高成長，算力成本考驗高估值

報告日期：2026.11.9 研究員：吳俞潔

10 月 26 日 Anthropic(ANT US) 向美國 SEC 提交 IPO 申請，11 月 2 日更
新第三季財報，市場預期公司將於 11 月底前上市，估值達 1.5 至 2 兆。
Anthropic 旗下 Claude 主要應用於企業 AI、 軟體開發及Agent 工作流程 ，
受惠 Claude Code 及企業 API 需求快速成長，26Q3 營收達 169.6 億(季增
47%)，2026 年前三季累計營收 332 億。目前普遍預估公司 2026 年營收有
望達 580 億 ， 若以Palantir 做為高成長的企業 AI 軟體作為基準，Anthropic
也受惠企業 AI 支出增加， 營收增速高於Palantir，但未來大量算力成本以及
現金流穩定度不如成熟軟體公司， 對應27 至 30 倍 Forward P/S，Anthropic 
IPO 估值達 1.6 至 1.8 兆，引發讓市場對企業 AI 商業化及模型公司估值的想
像空間。 
不過，兆豐認為 Anthropic 雖然已經證明企業願意為 AI 模型付費，但目前
1.6 至 1.8 兆估值已經提前反應未來數年營收維持高速成長，以及算力成本
持續改善的預期。公司 26Q3 營收季增 47%，但因算力及基礎設施費用季增
77%，導致營益率由上季 19%降至 15%，顯示收入增加並未帶來相同比例的
獲利成長。另外，公司已經簽訂 8,954 億的長期算力承諾，未來仍然需要擴
大 Token 需求才能支撐相關支出，考量企業 AI 需求持續成長，但模型價格
競爭及算力成本有壓縮利潤率風險， 因此兆豐目前對Anthropic 的 IPO 估值
持中性看待。

Claude Code 帶動企業 AI 需求，Anthropic 收入主要來自 Token 用量 
Anthropic 成立於 2021 年，由前 OpenAI 研究人員 Dario Amodei 創立，主
要開發 Claude 系列 AI 模型，透過 Claude 訂閱服務、企業 API 及 Claude 
Code 產品取得收入。相比傳統 SaaS 以固定訂閱費用為主，Anthropic 80%
營收來自 Token 使用量計價 ， 訂閱收入占20%， 企業可以透過AWS、Google 
Cloud 的雲端平台採購 Claude 服務，CSP 客戶占公司收入 48%，這種模式
使得 Anthropic 能隨著企業 AI 使用量增加而擴大收入，但也導致營收受到
Token 價格及企業使用量變動影響。 
受惠 Claude Code 及企業 API 需求成長，過去 12 個月支出超過 10 萬的客
戶達 11,900 家，其中超過 1,900 家年支出超過 100 萬，250 家超過 1,000

### Page 2

評等分級：買進 （預期未來六個月股價漲幅超過+30%）; 逢低買進 （預期未來六個月股價漲幅介於+15%與+30%之間）; 區間操作
（預期未來六個月股價漲跌幅介於-15%與+15%之間）; 逢高賣出 （預期未來六個月股價跌幅介於-15%與-30%之間） ；賣出 （預期
未來六個月股價跌幅超過-30%）。 
本刊所刊載之內容僅做為參考，惟已力求正確與完整，但因時間及市場客觀因素改變所造成產業、市場或個股之相關條件改變，投資人需自行考量
投資之需求與風險，本公司恕不負擔任何法律責任。任何轉載或引用本報告內容必須經本公司同意。  
106.4.14 版 
- 2 - 
海外產業訊息評論 
萬，企業 AI 支出正轉向更大規模的付費使用，也帶動 Anthropic 營收快速成
長。

估值達 1.8 兆，Token 價格決定高成長能否持續 
Anthropic 2026 年前三季營收達 332 億(年增 12 倍)，26Q3 營收 170 億(季
增 47%)，但相比 Q2 季增 144%成長大幅放緩。目前普遍預估公司 2026 年
營收達 580 億，若以 Palantir 作為高成長企業 AI 軟體的估值參考 ，合理倍數
落在 27 至 30 倍 Forward P/S，對應 IPO 估值 1.6 至 1.8 兆，兆豐認為雖然
Anthropic 營收成長的確遠高於多數軟體公司，Claude Code 及企業 API 的
高需求也支持未來營收持續擴張，但但模型訓練及推論需要持續投入大量算
力 ， 獲利率及現金流穩定度不如成熟軟體公司，以 2026 年 580 億營收估算，
隱含 28 至 31 倍 Forward P/S，反應了市場不只預期公司維持高成長，也預
期未來利潤率能隨著規模擴大而改善。 
Anthropic 80%收入來自 Token 用量計價 ， 未來營收不只取決於企業使用量 ，
也包含模型能否維持目前的高階定價， 外資8 月調查 100 名歐美大型企業 AI
預算決策者，92%預期未來 12 個月 Token 預算增加，但若 Token 價格上漲
50%，84%企業會優化使用或轉換其他模型，若價格下降 50%，86%企業則
願意增加使用量，反應企業 AI 需求仍有成長空間，但客戶對價格相當敏感，
尤其開放權重模型及低成本模型持續改善，企業有機會將部分工作轉向更為
便宜的模型，即使整體 Token 使用量增加，但開放權重和低成本模型競爭，
Anthropic 面臨單價競爭導致營收增速放緩的風險，因此兆豐認為目前 1.6 至
1.8 兆估值已經反應 Anthropic 長期維持高成長及較高 Token 價格的預期，
但如果模型競爭加劇，導致價格下降速度超過增加的使用量，將有估值下修
風險。

接近 9,000 億的算力承諾，收入成長未必同步轉為獲利 
Anthropic 為了支撐 Claude 模型訓練及企業推論需求 ， 持續擴大與Amazon、
Google、Microsoft 及其他 AI 基礎設施供應商的合作 ， 並且透過Nvidia GPU、
Google TPU 及 AWS Trainium 分散算力來源，Anthropic 目前即時可用算力
達 2.7GW，與 OpenAI 的 3.2GW 差距縮小，近期再透過 Nscale、Lambda
及 SpaceX 取得 1.1GW 的新增算力協議。不過，算力擴張也帶來龐大的長
期採購支出 ，Anthropic 已經簽訂 8,954 億的長期算力承諾 ， 其中4,475 億預
計在 2030 年底前兌現， 未來12 個月到期的承諾達 698 億，合約有最低採購
要求，即使未來需求不如預期，公司仍然需要負擔相關成本。 
從財務表現來看，Anthropic 26Q3 營收季增 47%，但算力及基礎設施費用季
增 77%，營益率也由 19%下降至 15%，顯示新增收入並未帶來相同比例的
獲利成長，雖然公司提到部分成本增加來自短期高價算力採購，未來隨著長
期合約及專用資料中心陸續上線，單位算力成本有機會下降，但兆豐認為即
使採購成本改善，也不代表利潤率一定回升，主要因 Anthropic 提前承諾大
規模算力支出，但未來 Token 收入仍然取決於企業實際使用量及模型定價，

### Page 3

評等分級：買進 （預期未來六個月股價漲幅超過+30%）; 逢低買進 （預期未來六個月股價漲幅介於+15%與+30%之間）; 區間操作
（預期未來六個月股價漲跌幅介於-15%與+15%之間）; 逢高賣出 （預期未來六個月股價跌幅介於-15%與-30%之間） ；賣出 （預期
未來六個月股價跌幅超過-30%）。 
本刊所刊載之內容僅做為參考，惟已力求正確與完整，但因時間及市場客觀因素改變所造成產業、市場或個股之相關條件改變，投資人需自行考量
投資之需求與風險，本公司恕不負擔任何法律責任。任何轉載或引用本報告內容必須經本公司同意。

若新增算力無法帶來足夠的 Token 收入 ， 固定採購成本將壓縮獲利及現金流。 
因此兆豐認為 Anthropic 目前估值不只反應營收持續高成長，也隱含未來算
力成本下降能帶動利潤率改善，但考量 26Q3 營收增速大幅放緩，營益率下
滑、未來長期算力採購承諾龐大，以及 Token 需求成長無法抵銷模型降價及
新增算力成本 ， 將壓縮利潤率 ， 不足以支撐更高的高成長成熟企業 AI 軟體公
司估值，因此兆豐對 1.6 至 1.8 兆的 IPO 估值持中性看待

（本資料僅供內部參考，不作為投資決策依據）

## Document 098: 2026年9月29日匯率利率資訊

Source PDF: all report pdf/2026年9月29日匯率利率資訊.pdf
Report source: Mega Bank (匯率利率資訊)
Date: 2026-09-29
Pages: 7

### Page 2

2  兆豐國際商業銀行 http://www.megabank.com.tw/
上週重要市場資訊
 澳洲 8 月就業人數勁增 3.95 萬人大幅優於預期，但勞動參與率上升至 67.1%，
推升失業率至 4.6%，呈現勞動力擴增但市場邊際降溫跡象。澳洲央行總裁布拉
克日前指出，失業率回升至5%有助於抑制頑固通膨。考量物價長期高於目標中
點，市場預期澳洲央行本週二將重啟緊縮，升息 1 碼至 4.60%，創 2011年底以
來新高。
 瑞士央行（SNB）上週四宣布連續第五次將關鍵政策利率維持在0%不變，符合
市場預期。儘管受油價推升使 8 月通膨微升至 0.8%，但仍處於目標區間，央行
預期中期通膨壓力溫和，現行極度寬鬆立場依然適當。決議公布後瑞郎兌美元跌
至 1.2077。相較全球主要央行相繼緊縮，瑞士央行維持超低利率，策略轉為視情
況調節匯市以穩定瑞郎。
 標普全球公布歐元區 9 月綜合 PMI 初值升至 53.1，創三年新高，製造與服務業
同步擴張，新訂單增速達四年多最快，印證經濟無懼美伊衝突引發的能源成本飆

升，展現超預期韌性。受能源價格傳導加速影響，通膨壓力再度升溫，儘管尚未
浮現薪資二輪效應，但強勁景氣數據促使市場大幅提高歐洲央行提前於10月例
會再度升息的押注。
 美國 9 月綜合 PMI 初值勁升至 58.4，創 62 個月新高；製造業與服務業 PMI 亦
逆勢走強至 57與 58.7，凸顯企業擴張強勁，引發市場對通膨持續性的擔憂。市
場焦點轉向 9 月 30日公布的美國 8 月 PCE、個人消費支出與 Q2 GDP終值，同
日日本亦將發布短觀調查與央行意見摘要。若終端消費延續強勁，將牽動美日央
行政策利率預期。

### Page 3

3  兆豐國際商業銀行 http://www.megabank.com.tw/ 
 美元兌新台幣匯率走勢  
Sep/29/2026

＜上週走勢＞ 
上週台幣匯價高點在 31.655，低點在 31.866，以 31.790作收，較前一周收盤價
升值 2.8分。 
上週台幣先升後貶，周初，台股創新高，外資連續五日買超台股並匯入，推升
台幣最高來到 31.655，不過逢低吸引進口商、政府基金等買盤進場，台幣主要在
31.700 附近震盪；臨近周末，美國 9 月綜合 PMI 由 8 月的 56 走升至 58.4，創下逾
5 年來的新高，加上美伊戰事持續，皆推升市場對後續升息的預期，美元指數升至
101 上方，台幣跳空開低，回貶至 31.800 上方，最低來到 31.866，不過隨後出口商
賣壓出籠，台幣跌勢放緩，最終在央行調節之下收在 31.790。 
＜未來分析＞ 
     預估本週台幣區間震盪。就國際市場而言，美國經濟表現仍具韌性，9月綜合
PMI 由 8 月的 56 上升至 58.4，企業活動加速至逾 5 年來最強，數據公布後美國 10
年債券殖利率升破 5%，Fed Watch 顯示市場對於聯準會 10 月升息預期升高，有助
於美元指數維持偏強格局，預計將給台幣帶來壓力；就台灣市場來看，台股在創下
新高後回落，而外資也結束連續五日買超的走勢，轉為賣超，短線上資金匯入不利
台幣表現，然而，台灣8 月外銷訂單達 1,029.6億美元、年增 71.4%，創歷史新高，
AI 需求帶動出口持續強勁，出口商後續拋匯需求預計將提供台幣支撐。綜合以上，
預估本週台幣區間震盪。 
＜預估本週＞ 美元兌台幣：31.600-32.000

### Page 4

4  兆豐國際商業銀行 http://www.megabank.com.tw/ 
美元兌人民幣匯率走勢  
Sep/29/2026 
＜上週走勢＞ 
上週人民幣震盪走貶。人民幣中間價在上周連續十天走升，並升破6.7500整數
關口，最高來到 6.7468，帶動人民幣偏升，不過由於美伊戰事持續，市場對於聯準
會升息預期提升，美元指數震盪走強，加上週間公布美國9月綜合 PMI由 8 月的 56
走升至 58.4，創下逾 5 年來的新高，美元指數突破 101 整數關卡，令境外人民幣承
壓，USD/CNH回貶至 6.7200上方，終場境外人民幣收在6.7227，週線貶值283點，
境內人民幣則收在 6.7125，週線貶值 141 點。

＜未來分析＞ 
     預估本週人民幣高檔震盪。就國際市場來看，美中兩國完成新一輪談判，美方
表示雙方同意將原訂 11 月 10 日到期的貿易休戰延長至明年 1 月 10 日，並持續就
關稅、貿易及供應鏈等議題進行協商，降低短期貿易摩擦升溫的風險，對人民幣形
成一定支撐。不過，美國近期經濟數據仍具韌性，加以聯準會偏鷹立場推升美元，
預計將限制人民幣升值空間；就中國國內市場來看，近期人行持續偏強設定中間價，
帶動人民幣維持偏強走勢，惟美中利差再度擴大，預計將限制人民幣升值的空間；
最後就技術面來看，USD/CNH日線圖中，匯價反彈至 20日均線之上，顯示原先下
行動能暫歇，上方壓力先看向季線 6.7350附近，綜合以上，預估本週人民幣高檔震
盪。

＜預估本週＞ 美元兌人民幣：6.6900-6.7400

### Page 5

5  兆豐國際商業銀行 http://www.megabank.com.tw/ 
美元兌日圓匯率走勢  
         Sep/29/2026

＜上週走勢＞ 
上週 USD/JPY先上後下。週初，美國多位聯準會官員鷹派談話，帶動美元指數
持續上行，推升 USD/JPY延續前一週的走勢來到 157上方；週間，受美國 PMI 強
於預期與美債殖利率全面上行帶動，市場重新提高對聯準會後續升息路徑的押注，
帶動 USD/JPY大漲突破 159的關卡；臨近週末，日本首相高市早苗表態日圓被低估
是個問題，使市場提高對政策訊號與潛在干預的敏感度，帶動USD/JPY回吐大部分
漲幅，終場收在 157.26，週線上漲 56點。 
＜未來分析＞ 
  預估本週 USD/JPY區間震盪，主要原因如下：一、日本財務大臣片山皋月上週
五罕見透露，美國總統川普在美日元首峰會上對日圓過度疲弱表達關切，首相高市
早苗亦回應日圓遭低估確屬問題。儘管近期美元指數維持在 101 上方，但在日本政
府可能干預的陰影下，預計將限縮日圓貶值的空間。二、美國9 月綜合 PMI產出指
數創逾五年新高，疊加美伊戰事推升油價突破100美元與柴油大漲，通膨壓力劇增。
美聯儲理事巴爾表態可能需再升息兩次，芝加哥聯儲古爾斯比也示警能源衝擊具持
續性，推升市場押注 10 月升息機率升至六成，預計將支撐 USD/JPY 走勢。三、就
技術面而言，日線圖中，USD/JPY連日上漲一度站回年線但隨即失守，目前依舊承
壓於季線及年線下方，不過各天期均線趨平，且下方尚有月線支撐，短線尚無明確
的方向。綜合以上，預估 USD/JPY本週呈現區間震盪的可能性較高。 
＜預估本週＞ 美元兌日幣：155.30-159.30 
前週交易區間 
156.56 159.03

6  兆豐國際商業銀行 http://www.megabank.com.tw/ 
美元兌歐元匯率走勢  
             Sep/29/2026

＜上週走勢＞ 
  上週 EUR/USD 下跌。週初，歐元延續前一週偏弱的走勢，加上聯準會官員鷹
派發言，導致歐元跌破 1.1450的重要關卡；週間，儘管油價自高檔回落，但市場解
讀為能源成本仍處高位、難以迅速回落，推升對美國實質利率與利差優勢的預期。
加上歐洲在能源依賴與成長動能較弱下，相對於美國更難承受長期高利率環境，歐
元最低來到 1.1358；臨近週末，歐元跌深反彈，終場收在1.1391，週線下跌 91點。 
＜未來分析＞ 
  預估本週 EUR/USD 震盪偏弱，主要原因如下：一、歐洲央行首席經濟學家連
恩示警，第二波能源價格飆升將推升通膨，預計至2027年中期始回落至目標水準。
德國央行月報指出，受中東衝突、低天然氣庫存及明年醫療改革影響，通膨將居高
不下；夏季雖因出口疲弱與乾旱致增長趨緩，但在製造業回暖與財政支出挹注下，
估年底經濟有望重啟復甦，預計將提供歐元一定的支撐。二、美聯儲官員接連釋放
強烈鷹派信號，聖路易聯儲總裁穆薩萊姆直言當前 3.75%-4.00%利率仍偏寬鬆，呼
籲盡早收緊；芝加哥聯儲總裁古爾斯比亦警告，AI投資繁榮引發需求過熱，疊加持
續性供應衝擊，可能迫使美聯儲加速且更積極地升息。目前Fedwatch顯示預期明年
4 月前可能再升息三次，將不利歐元表現。三、技術面而言，在日線圖中，EUR/USD
在各天期均線下方，且接連失守1.15及 1.14整數關卡，空方動能較佔優勢。綜合以
上，預估 EUR/USD本週呈現震盪偏弱的可能性較高。 
＜預估本週＞ 歐元兌美元：1.1300-1.1450 
前週交易區間 
1.1358 1.1495

7  兆豐國際商業銀行 http://www.megabank.com.tw/ 
                           新台幣利率走勢分析

115.09.21-115.09.24 
銀行隔夜拆款利率 0.804% ~ 0.830%  
30天期短票利率   1.65% ~ 1.69%  
公債－10 年期-A15109     1.95%  
－ 5 年期- A15108 1.84%

一、 台幣貨幣市場部分，本周央行持續每日減發定存單，令市場資金充裕，帶動短
率靠向區間低位。 
週二央行標售 2 年期定存單 250 億元，加權平均得標利率為 1.638%，略低於
市場預期，且較上次標售結果下跌 2.5個基本點。 
週四隔夜拆款利率區間在 0.804%~0.830%， 30 天期短票主要成交在
1.65%~1.69%，利率較上週下跌 1 個基本點。 
二、 本週市場交投冷清，10 年期指標券 A15109 及 5 年期指標券 A15108 均未成
交，週四等殖系統參考報價中價分別為 1.95%、1.84%。 
台股排擠效應、國際債市走勢偏空，加上油價續處高檔，通膨潛在威脅仍存，
12 月升息可能性未能完全排除，市場氣氛續偏觀望，預期台債殖利率將維持
高檔區間盤整 ， 預估5年券區間為 1.84%~1.90%，10年券區間為 1.95%~2.0%。

## Document 141: 261105_daiwa_vpec-UG

Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

Taiwan Information Technology

What’s new: Following VPEC’s robust 3Q26 earnings release on 30 
October and our subsequent market research, we raise our forecasts and 
12M TP, premised upon: 1) a higher top-line growth, thanks to its faster 
capacity conversion to meet strong demand for AI optical communication 
(OC) devices; and 2) a higher substrate supply consigned by customers, 
helping margin. Our thesis remains intact for VPEC to enjoy strong growth 
from its share gains in the AI OC market, with broadened customer base 
and applications. Given increased jitters on AI bubble and macro issues 
post the US mid-term elections, we upgrade our rating one notch to Buy (1) 
and suggest investors take advantage of market volatility to bargain-hunt.

What’s the impact: Forecast changes. We raise our 2026-27E EPS by 3-
4% to factor in VPEC’s robust 3Q26 results and higher revenue growth in 
4Q26, on our latest market research. This is premised upon: 1) faster-than-
expected capacity conversion from GaAs for mobile to InP for AI OC at its 
MOCVD reactors to mitigate supply constraints and meet more optical 
demand; and 2) increased InP substrate supply consigned by customers, 
helping to increase GM. As such, we expect VPEC’s earnings growth to 
remain strong through 2027, supporting its hefty valuation.

Speeding up capacity conversion to meet more demand. Given a 
limited floor space in its Fab 2, VPEC may accommodate 70+ MOCVD 
reactors in 2027 (currently: 65). Yet, given still-suboptimal utilisation in its 
RF business due to weak consumer demand for smartphones on cost 
inflation, VPEC is accelerating capacity conversion to accommodate strong 
AI OC demand, in order to narrow the supply/demand gap.

Increasing substrate consignment to benefit GM. Due to the InP 
substrate supply constraint and strong AI demand, customers with strong 
purchasing power are increasing substrate consignment to VPEC, adding 
to GM since the consigned materials are excluded from the GM calculation. 
We continue to expect the company to register AI-related revenue, 
including PD, LD and HAMR, with broadened customer base, of TWD1.2-
1.3bn this year, TWD3bn in 2027E and at least TWD4bn in 2028E, from 
TWD550m in 2025 (see our preview note, 8 September).

What we recommend: We raise our 12M TP for VPEC to TWD555 
(previous: TWD535), still based on our 4-quarter-forward PER of 58x. We 
upgrade our rating by one notch to Buy (1), with key downside risk of 
China’s export control inducing any unexpected supply chain hiccups.

How we differ: Our 2027-28E EPS are 14-36% below Bloomberg 
consensus, as we believe the street is too aggressive on VPEC’s AI ramp.

5 November 2026

Upgrading: value resurfaces after jitters rise

➢ Raising forecasts on both higher top-line and margin trajectories 
➢ Intact optical ramp in AI connectivity despite market volatility 
➢ Upgrading our rating to Buy (1) with a higher 12M TP of TWD555

Company profile 
Founded in 1996, Visual Photonics Epitaxy (VPEC) is a veteran epiwafer maker in Taiwan. The 
company started with growing epiwafers for LED applications and then transitioned to microwave 
communications. Strategically moving up the technology verticals, in recent years VPEC has tapped into 
the high-value-add applications of optical sensing & communications for both blended ASP and margin 
accretion.

### Page 5

5

Visual Photonics Epitaxy (2455 TT): 5 November 2026 
ESG analysis

Update Date: 5 Nov 2026 
Source: Daiwa, Company

ESG risks
Risks Management Analyst comments
G
Executive/board
quality 2
VPEC's board of directors (BoD) has 11 members, including 3 independent directors,
representing 27% of total directors in 2023, which is slightly higher than the regulatory
requirement but lower than at other semiconductor companies under our coverage. Its chairman
and general manager are not the same person and most of the directors are not executive
management. In addition to the annual internal performance evaluation of the BoD, VPEC also
requests an external evaluation of its BoD at least once every 3 years. We believe this helps
strengthen the functionalities of the BoD despite a relatively low percentage of independent
directors.
Capital management 1
In the past 10 years, the company’s payout ratio has been above 78%. In terms of the dollar
amount, its cash dividends were TWD2.3 per share in 2017-23. We view VPEC has well-
managed dividend policy and see its dividend payout ratio is appropriate.
Related party &
transaction 1 The company does not have any meaningful related-party transactions.
S Supply chain management 2
VPEC has set up a "Supplier Management Procedure (M0047)" and evaluates its suppliers in
terms of environmental management, compliance, and green products. The company audits its
suppliers to ensure a good performance in environmental sustainability, health & safety and
corporate social responsibility.
E Water & wastewater management 2 It has appointed professionals according to regulations to conduct environmental management of air pollution, waste water, waste and hazardous substances. Also, it has introduced the ISO14001 environmental management system and passed the SGS certification in 2002.
E
Waste & hazardous
materials
management
3
VPEC conducts audits on companies that handle the company’s environmentally sensitive waste
disposal to ensure the fulfilment of environmental sustainability and the implementation of
corporate social responsibility.
E GHG emissions 3 It has set a target to reduce its GHG emissions and energy consumption per unit of product by 5% per year and verify greenhouse gas inventories bt ISO 14064 regulations.
Note: Management score represents a company's ability to manage/benefit from certain ESG topics. The scores range from 1 to 3, with 1 being the strongest.

### Page 3

Alchip (3661.TW)
07 November 2026 Citi Research
3
AI infrastructure demand, supply constraints and 
engineering productivity are key for growth outlook
Despite rising concerns over data center power availability, infrastructure 
deployment delays and potential AI investment moderation, management reported 
no meaningful slowdown in customer demand or changes in development 
schedules. Based on discussions with major customers, Alchip expects strong 
demand extending through 2027–2029, with next-generation AI accelerator 
volumes potentially exceeding current-generation levels substantially. 
Management cautioned that shipment delays could arise from supply-side 
constraints, particularly PCB, substrates, passive components and advanced 
semiconductor capacity, rather than weakening end demand. More importantly,  
large AI accelerator projects generally receive priority allocation, leaving smaller 
customers and traditional applications more exposed to shortages. Meanwhile, AI 
adoption is materially improving Alchip’s engineering productivity, particularly in 
RTL coding, verification, design iteration and analysis of implementation reports. 
Alchip’s CPU and networking projects are progressing on schedule, but 
management believes advanced AI accelerator designs offer substantially greater 
engineering differentiation and revenue opportunities than conventional CPU 
projects.
Looking into 2028, the key limitation could shift from customer demand to Alchip’s 
ability to finance and support larger wafer procurement commitments. 
Consequently, working-capital requirements, customer payment terms and the 
potential need for additional financing will become increasingly important factors 
in determining the company’s achievable revenue scale and profitability. 
We lift our 2027 /2028 earnings projection for Alchip by 14% and 13%, reflecting 
stronger order allocation into next two years. We are maintaining our Buy on the 
stock but lowering our TP to NT$6,000, with lower PER at 27x of 2027 EPS (from 
35x previously), reflecting macro and AI policy uncertainty, in the low end of its 
historical PER.

### Page 7

Alchip (3661.TW)
07 November 2026 Citi Research
7
Alchip
Company description
Alchip is a leading provider of silicon design and manufacturing service,
specializing in ASIC (Application-Specific Integrated Circuit) design,
development, and production. Founded in 2003, the company has >1,000
employees and operates in Taipei, Shanghai, Japan, Korea, and the US. Alchip
serves a wide range of industries, including automotive, industrials, consumer
electronics, and computing.

Investment strategy
We rate Alchip as Buy. We expect Alchip to deliver a strong snapback in
2026/27 with its N3 project ramping up. Moreover, we are also positive over
the long-term growth opportunity from its AI ASIC projects with its major CSP
client. Alchip remains one of the few design service providers within TSMC’s
3DFabric alliance. In our view, if CSPs work more aggressively on their internal
design and become more competitive on product offerings, Alchip would be

well positioned as a back-end/turnkey partner.
Valuation
Our target price for Alchip of NT$6,000 is based on 27x 2027E EPS. 27x target
PER is lower than its historical 3-year PER range, which we see as justified on
account of strong growth potential of its AI ASIC projects with its key CSP
client, reflecting macro and AI policy uncertainty but with better project cycle
and strong growth momentum resuming next year. Our target price equates
to 2026/27E P/B of 6x/5x.

Guide to Citi Research Fundamental Research Investment Ratings:
Citi Research stock recommendations include an investment rating and an optional risk rating to highlight high risk stocks.

Risk rating takes into account both price volatility and fundamental criteria. Stocks will either have no risk rating or a High risk
rating assigned.
Investment Ratings: Citi Research investment ratings are Buy, Neutral and Sell. Our ratings are a function of analyst
expectations of expected total return ("ETR") and risk. ETR is the sum of the forecast price appreciation (or depreciation) plus
the dividend yield for a stock within the next 12 months.  The target price is based on a 12 month time horizon. The Investment

rating definitions are: Buy (1) ETR of 15% or more or 25% or more for High risk stocks; and Sell (3) for negative ETR. Any covered
stock not assigned a Buy or a Sell is a Neutral (2). For stocks rated Neutral (2), if an analyst believes that there are insufficient
valuation drivers and/or investment catalysts to derive a positive or negative investment view, they may elect with the approval
of Citi Research management not to assign a target price and, thus, not derive an ETR. Citi Research may suspend its rating

and target price and assign “Rating Suspended” status for regulatory and/or internal policy reasons. Citi Research may also
suspend its rating and target price and assign "Under Review" status for other exceptional circumstances (e.g. lack of
information critical to the analyst's thesis, trading suspension) affecting the company and/or trading in the company's
securities. In both such situations, the rating and target price will show as “—“ and “-“ respectively in the rating history price

chart. Prior to 11 April 2022 Citi Research assigned “Under Review” status to both situations and prior to 11 Nov 2020 only in
exceptional circumstances. As soon as practically possible, the analyst will publish a note re-establishing a rating and
investment thesis. Investment ratings are determined by the ranges described above at the time of initiation of coverage, a
change in investment and/or risk rating, or a change in target price (subject to limited management discretion). At times, the

expected total returns may fall outside of these ranges because of market price movements and/or other short-term volatility
or trading patterns. Such interim deviations will be permitted but will become subject to review by Research Management.
Your decision to buy or sell a security should be based upon your personal investment objectives and should be made only
after evaluating the stock's expected performance and risk.
Catalyst Watch/Short Term Views (“STV”) Ratings Disclosure:

Catalyst Watch and STV Upside/Downside calls: Citi Research may also include a Catalyst Watch or STV Upside or Downside
call to indicate the analyst expects the share price to rise (fall) in absolute terms over a specified period of 30 or 90 days in
reaction to one or more specific near-term catalysts or events impacting the company or the market. A Catalyst Watch will be
published when Analyst confidence is high that an impact to share price will occur; it will be a STV when confidence level is

moderate. A Catalyst Watch or STV Upside/Downside call will automatically expire at the end of the specified 30/90 day
period. The Catalyst Watch will also be automatically removed if share price performance (calculated at market close) exceeds
15% against the direction of the call (unless over-ridden by the analyst). The analyst may also remove a Catalyst Watch or STV
call prior to the end of the specified period in a published research note. A Catalyst Watch/STV Upside or Downside call may be

our ratings distribution table for our Catalyst Watch/STV Upside/Downside rating system. However, we reiterate that we do
not consider No View to be a recommendation. For all Catalyst Watch/STV Upside/Downside calls, risk exists that the
catalyst(s) and associated share-price movement will not materialize as expected.

Delta Electronics Inc.  | Asia Pacific
October  Sales +14% MoM/
+30% YoY
In this report, we focus on Delta's monthly sales, which we
believe could be a catalyst for its share price.
Details:
• October sales came in at NT$74,666mn (+14% MoM/+30% YoY), driven by
the increasing shipment of power supply units, HVDC power racks, and liquid
cooling products.
• QTD revenue reached 35% of MSe at NT$215,881mn (+10% QoQ/+34% YoY),
and 34% of consensus at NT$219,806mn (+12% QoQ/+36% YoY).

Our view:
• We think 4Q26 revenue is tracking slightly ahead of market expectations.
• We think the slightly better Oct monthly sales should help on near-term
sentiment, though more meaningful upside to market expectations on
earnings growth might  depend on the penetration of HVDC power racks into
the coming years.
• We continue to see Delta as a key beneficiary of the increasing power
consumption of AI clusters through its comprehensive power/cooling

offerings. Stay OW.

Valuation Methodology and Risks
Delta Electronics Inc. (2308.TW)
Base case: P/E multiple of 30x based on average 2027-28e EPS.                          We believe our target multiple
is justified by the 59% earnings CAGR we estimate for 2025-28, implying a PEG of 0.5, versus
the stock's ~23x P/E and 23% earnings CAGR during 2022-25, which implied a PEG of 1.0.
Risks to Upside
n Faster-than-expected global economic growth

n Falling raw material prices
n Margin improvement
Risks to Downside
n Global economic slowdown
n Ongoing component supply tightness
n Rising raw material prices
n Margin contraction as a result of higher operating expenses or faster ASP erosion

Analyst Stock Ratings
Overweight (O). The stock's total return is expected to exceed the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next
12-18 months.
Equal-weight (E). The stock's total return is expected to be in line with the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over

the next 12-18 months.
Not-Rated (NR). Currently the analyst does not have adequate conviction about the stock's total return relative to the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage
universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next 12-18 months.
Underweight (U). The stock's total return is expected to be below the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next

12-18 months.
Unless otherwise specified, the time frame for price targets included in Morgan Stanley Research is 12 to 18 months.
Analyst Industry Views
Attractive (A): The analyst expects the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months to be attractive vs. the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated
below.
In-Line (I): The analyst expects the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months to be in line with the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated below.

Cautious (C): The analyst views the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months with caution vs. the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated below.
Benchmarks for each region are as follows: North America - S&P 500; Latin America - relevant MSCI country index or MSCI Latin America Index; Europe - MSCI Europe; Japan - TOPIX; Asia -

## Document 196: 261110_ml_vanguard

Source PDF: all report pdf/261110_ml_vanguard.pdf
Report source: line報告備份
Date: 2026-11-10

### Page 1

Vanguard International Semiconductor Co

Robust 8" demand and pricing with solid 
12" fab ramp

Reiterate Rating: BUY | PO: 250.00 TWD | Price: 132.00 TWD

3Q sales/margin supported by PMIC strength  
Vanguard's 3Q26 sales rose 10.2% QoQ on the back of increased wafer shipments (up 
5% QoQ), particularly for server and smartphone PMIC, and rising wafer prices (up 3% 
QoQ). As higher utilization of around 90% and a more favorable pricing and product mix 
more than offset rising manufacturing costs, the 3Q26 gross margin improved by 130bps 
QoQ, to 33.6%, bringing 3Q26 EPS to NT$1.62 (vs. BofAe/Street of NT$1.67/1.58). 
4Q capacity bottleneck offset by ASP uplift 
The company guided to 4Q26 sales growth of 5% QoQ at the midpoint, driven by a 1% 
QoQ increase in wafer shipments, with strong demand capped to some degree by tight 
capacity, and 4% higher ASPs QoQ, the stronger pricing reflecting rising costs. Assuming 
further utilization improvement above 90% amid this pricing strength, Vanguard expects 
the 4Q26 gross margin to expand to 35% at the midpoint, in line with BofAe.  
Solid 8" growth and smooth 12" ramp drive ’27-28 growth 
We believe Vanguard’s 8-inch utilization is on track to improve to the mid- to high 90s 
(%) in 1H27 and remain fully loaded from 2H27 on solid demand related to power IC for 
computing applications, while 12-inch VSMC should enter mass production from 1Q27, 
with utilization staying above 90% as capacity gradually expand s to 44k WPM by 4Q28. 
Given the solid commitment from TSMC (interposer demand), the 12-inch margin profile 
should fare better than previously expected. This, coupled with an 8-inch price hike amid 
tight supply should drive GM expansion to 36% in 2027 (vs. 32.8% in 2026).  
Decent EPS CAGR of 72% supports high valuation  
Our EPS forecasts are largely unchanged at NT$ 6.17 in 2026, NT$10.33 in 2027, and 
NT$18.15 in 2028. We reiterate our Buy rating and PO of NT$25 0, still based on 17x 
2H27-1H28e EPS (vs. the stock’s historical 10-32x range) as a benign 8-inch supply-
demand landscape and smooth 12-inch ramp-up support a 45% sales and 72% earnings 
CAGR over 2026-28e.

Key Metrics      
* For full definitions of iQmethod SM measures, see page 6.

Company Sector 
Semiconductors

Company Description 
Vanguard International Semiconductor Co is a pure 8-inch 
foundry headquartered in Hsinchu, Taiwan. The company 
has five fabs, one of which is in Singapore. Collectively, the 
capacity is now about 280k WPM, and the company focuses 
on fabricating power semiconductors and driver IC for both 
IDMs (integrated device manufacturers) and fabless 
customers.

Investment Rationale 
We have a Buy rating on Vanguard as we expect an 
improvement in the overall 8-inch foundry supply/demand 
landscape from TSMC's retirement of some legacy capacity 
and limited new industry capacity additions. The higher 
utilization and pricing upcycle in 8-inch, coupled with 
enhanced mix from market share gains in Power IC, are 
likely to support sales and gross margin expansion.

### Page 4

Price objective basis & risk 
Vanguard International Semiconductor Co (VGILF) 
Our PO of NT$250 is based on 17x 2H27-1H28 EPS. This target multiple is slightly 
below the midpoint of Vanguard's historical range of 10-32x, reflecting both 
depreciation associated with the Singapore fab and Vanguard's increasing share in the 
Power IC market amid a utilization and pricing uptrend.

Downside risks: 
(1) Higher cost burden if customers demand to move the production base to US and/or 
Europe. 
(2) Slowdown of growth in auto market where the company has 10-20% revenue 
exposure.

Upside risks: 
(1) Stronger-than-expected end market demand for power management IC (PMIC) and/or 
display driver IC (DDIC). 
(2) Expedited market share gain due to the order shift by customers from China-based 
foundries.

RA 배현서 
hyeonseo.bae@meritz.co.kr 배당은 지켜질 것  
3Q26 지배주주 순이익, 컨센서스 -38% 하회 
매출 31.6조원 (+10% YoY) 으로 컨센서스에 부합했다. 출고 판매 (+10% YoY), 소
매 판매 (+8% YoY) 모두 높은 성장을 기록하며, 외형 확대에 기여했다. 
영업이익 1.82조원 (+24% YoY) 으로 컨센서스를 -25% 하회했다. 전년 동기 대미 
관세 25% 부과에 따른 기저효과 +5,330억원 (2025년 11월부터 15%로 하향)이 
존재했고 기말 환율 (원/달러 -12% QoQ 강세) 변동에 따른 판매보증 충당부채 환
입 +6,700억원을 인식했음에도 불구하고, 신규 출시 저가 BEV 모델들의 판매 확대
에 따른 전사 평균 판매가격 하락  할인 경쟁 심화  원재료 및 운송비 상승으로 부
진한 실적을 기록했다. 
지배주주 순이익 1.29조원 (-10% YoY) 으로 컨센서스를 -38% 하회했다. 영업 실적 
부진과 함께 원화 강세에 의한 외화자산 평가손실 및 관계사 실적 악화에 따른 지분
법 이익 감소 (-36% YoY)가 더해진 결과다.

6,000원 이상 배당 유지 전망 
4Q26 이후 실적 방향도 먹구름이 가득하다. 원/달러 환율의 YoY 강세 반영이 시작
된다. 미국 시장 내 경쟁력 확보를 위해 가장 중요한 원/엔 환율의 방향성 또한 비우
호적 흐름이 지속 중이다. 내수 시장을 포함해 전세계 주요 시장 내 중국산 차량과의 
경쟁 강도도 높아지고 있다. 원유 가격 뿐만 아니라 알루미늄, 구리 등 핵심 원재료 
가격의 상승 반영도 이어지고 있다. 
2026년 연간 지배주주 순이익 6.9조원 (-9% YoY) 을 전망한다. 주주환원 총액은 축
소될 것이다. 다만 자사주 매입을 줄이더라도 배당은 전년 수준을 지킬 것으로 기대
한다. 참고로 2025년 실적에 기반한 주주환원은 자사주 매입 0.9조원, 배당 2.6조원 
(주당 6,800원)이었으며, 주주환원율은 48%였다. 현대차그룹은 Boston Dynamics
에 대한 증자를 이어가고 있다. 정의선 회장도 마찬가지다. 그리고 기아의 배당은 현
대차  현대글로비스의 배당과 함께 증자 재원으로 활용되고 있다. 6,000 원 이상의 
배당 유지를 기대하며, 6% 이상 시가 배당 수익률 제시를 통해 주가 하방 경직성 확
보가 가능하다고 판단한다. 기업가치 상승 재개를 위해서는 기존 차량 제조  판매 사
업을 넘어 피지컬 데이터 디바이스 제공 사업 진출 가시성 확보가 필요하다. 
 Meritz Research  2026. 11. 2

Buy (20거래일 평균종가 대비 상승 여력 기준)

### Page 1

The dollar should stay supported by strong US growth, high
inﬂation and a Fed threatening to tighten before weakening
next spring as inﬂation falls. An AI correction looms large, and
what about attractive overseas growth drawing funds away
from the dollar?
Chris Turner
Better news from the AI sector is softening a key threat to the dollar
How to read our three calls for 2027

until next spring

### Page 2

Our base call assumes that the Federal Reserve stays
hawkish in the ﬁrst quarter, as decent US growth and above-
3% inﬂation keep money markets pricing in the threat of an
extended tightening cycle. That turns in the second quarter,
when energy-driven base eﬀects drop out of headline
inﬂation and a return to the Fed’s 2% inﬂation target sees
short-dated US rates move meaningfully lower.
That timing also suits events in Europe. French budgetary
politics look set to keep a risk premium in the euro ahead of

April's elections. We therefore see EUR/USD trading oﬀered in
a 1.10-15 range over the next four to ﬁve months. From the
second quarter, however, softer US rates, moderating energy
prices and clarity on French politics could trigger a gentle
recovery in EUR/USD towards 1.18 by year-end 2027.
Our risky call: AI bust
Energy and AI have been the dominant global macro drivers this
year and could well shape FX markets again in 2027. An AI bust is
not the base case, but if it occurred, it would lead to a lower dollar.

The IMF estimates the dollar could fall 6% under such a scenario.
The nature of the dollar decline could be erratic, however. If the
functioning of the ﬁnancial system were to come into question,
such as dollar liquidity drying up again, the dollar could ﬁrst rally.
But the inevitable Fed support measures to address liquidity needs
and the likely large rate cuts would ultimately push the dollar a lot
lower.
Our bold call: Overseas growth surprises

positively
EUR/USD rallied sharply in 2017 when Dutch and French elections
saw euro-sceptic parties failing to advance. Having toned down her
views on France leaving the euro, a decisive victory by Marine Le Pen
in April and greater budget clarity could reassure markets. If
eurozone growth continues to defy energy and political headwinds,

## Document 386: Financial conditions in the Eurozone_ a tightening temporarily tempered by credit volumes (2026-10-07)

Source PDF: all report pdf/Financial conditions in the Eurozone_ a tightening temporarily tempered by credit volumes (2026-10-07).pdf
Report source: BNP Paribas
Date: 2026-10-07
Pages: 3
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

7 October 2026
The bank
for a changing
world
FINANCIAL CONDITIONS IN THE EUROZONE: A TIGHTENING TEMPORARILY TEMPERED BY CREDIT VOLUMES
Thomas Humblot
*LATEST OBSERVATION: 5 OCTOBER 2026  **ESTIMATE
Since June 2026, the ECB has been publishing 1 a new indicator that helps to assess the transmission of its monetary policy to the economy:
the Broad Intermediation Gauge 2 (ECB-BIG). This new tool has revealed that, thus far, strong credit growth has limited the restrictive impact

of tightening financial conditions caused by rising interest rates in the Eurozone. However, this offsetting effect is beginning to fade.
The ECB-BIG: a new indicator for a broader view of financial conditions
The ECB-BIG complements the range of indicators used by the European Central Bank (ECB) to assess the effective transmission of its monetary
policy. This new tool provides a comprehensive view of how restrictive (or expansive) financial conditions are in the Eurozone. It stands out

for its approach that focuses more specifically on intermediation conditions. Therefore, the ECB-BIG provides a more comprehensive view than
most other financial condition indicators of financial conditions. In fact, it covers variables relating to the volume of bank lending to households
and businesses (the ‘volume’ component). Traditionally, this type of indicator tends to focus more on market variables (primarily interest and
exchange rates), share prices and corporate spreads. In addition, the ‘price’ component of the ECB-BIG is extended to include the cost of bank

loans, which is another new feature.
The rise in interest rates was temporarily offset by increased lending
By aggregating the variables relating to banking and non-banking intermediation conditions, the ECB-BIG shows that rising interest rates
(negative price effect) were offset in particular by lending volumes, which have continued to rise in 2026 (positive volume effect). A more
‘traditional’ indicator of financial conditions, the Macro-Finance FCI (MF-FCI) — which is, incidentally, included in the ECB-BIG — reveals a

much more pronounced tightening over the same period (see the orange curve in the chart). However, this offsetting of the price effect by the
volume effect is now beginning to wane, a sign that monetary policy is being effectively transmitted to the economy. Financial intermediaries
are gradually passing on the rise in rates to their customers (see the dotted green lines, which represent the composite indicators of the cost
of new loans to households and businesses, combining interest rates for different maturities), while lending volumes are falling. As a result,

between July and August 2026, seasonally adjusted flows of loans to households and businesses almost halved.
1 P . Lane, 2026, The Outlook for the Euro Area Economy, Background slides for Reuters NEXT Europe, London, 16 June 2026
2 A. Allayioti et al., 2026, The ECB-BIG index: tracking credit conditions in the euro area, ECB Economic Bulletin, Issue 5/2026
SOURCE: ALLAYIOTI AND AL. (2026), ECB, BLETZINGER ET AL. (2026), BNP PARIBAS ESTIMATES

In the longer term, financial conditions are expected to tighten, including as measured by the ECB-BIG
Despite the very sharp widening of sovereign spreads between 28 September and 2 October, financial conditions as measured by the
MF-FCI did not tighten, with sovereign yields actually falling over this period (with the exception of the OAT and the BTP), for example.

Nevertheless, the high volatility of the bond market, particularly in the sovereign segment, is likely to continue to affect financial
conditions. Looking slightly further ahead, the ECB’s key interest rate rises — the one that occurred on 16 September and another that we
anticipate for December 2026 (both +25 bp) — are likely to result in a more pronounced tightening. With a time lag of a number of weeks,
financial conditions, as defined by the ECB-BIG, are also expected to tighten. The price effect is likely to lead to a negative volume effect

on this occasion.

## Document 396: GLOBAL MARKETS CALL_ Air Coming Out Of AI Bubble

Source PDF: all report pdf/GLOBAL MARKETS CALL_ Air Coming Out Of AI Bubble.pdf
Report source: researchmail轉寄
Date: 2026-11-08
Pages: 8
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

From:    Yardeni QuickTakes <yardeni-research@ghost.io>
Subject: GLOBAL MARKETS CALL: Air Coming Out Of AI Bubble
Date:    1 hour ago
GLOBAL MARKETS CALL: Air Coming Out Of AI Bubble

YARDENI QUICKTAKES
GLOBAL MARKETS CALL: Air
Coming Out Of AI Bubble
By Ed Yardeni & Toby Hearst • 8 Nov 2026
View in browser
Photo by Gaël Gaborel - OrbisTerrae / Unsplash

### Page 2

The AI selloff went global last week. In the US, Democrats swept
Congress in the midterm elections on Tuesday, November 3, surfing on a
huge wave of voter hostility toward new data centers. On Thursday,
investors dumped the AI trade worldwide, and Asia’s chipmakers took the
brunt of the wipeout. Overseas earnings are still climbing, but valuations
keep falling. We see the AI backlash as a speed bump, not a roadblock, at
home and abroad. Here’s more:

I. Stock Market Performance
Global sector leadership flipped this week according to the FTSE indexes
(chart). Defensive sectors held up best, with Real Estate up 2.2% and
Consumer Staples up 0.7%, while Consumer Discretionary led with a
2.7% gain. Technology (-6.7%) and Telecommunications (-7.4%) were
near the bottom. Energy was the worst performer, down 10.4%, as fears
of a post-midterm re-escalation in the Middle East faded. The FTSE World
index fell 2.5%.

The US has been gaining ground since we lowered our "Go Global"
investment strategy to market weight on October 18. The US MSCI’s
share of the All Country World MSCI’s market cap has climbed to 65.3%,
up from 62.4% in March (chart). The gains have come at the expense of
everyone else. The Emerging Markets MSCI’s share has dropped to
11.1% from 12.1% in mid-October.

### Page 3

The AI-heavy markets were at the bottom of the country ETF rankings this
week (chart). Taiwan fell 11.7% in dollars, South Korea 10.4%, and Japan
5.0%. This year’s laggards caught bids. South Africa led with an 8.5%
gain, followed by India at 6.6%. The US ETF fell 2.7%, slightly worse than
the All Country World ex-US ETF’s 1.8% decline.
Both the US and overseas ETFs have pulled back from their recent highs
(chart). Both remain in solid uptrends that began in late 2022.

### Page 4

II. Stock Market Earnings & Valuation
The All Country World ex-US MSCI’s forward earnings rose to yet another
record high this week (chart). It has soared since 2025 after going
nowhere for most of the previous decade.
Forward earnings is up 41.0% y/y, while forward revenues is up 11.2%
(chart). During solid economic expansions, forward revenue growth tends
to hover around 5%. Today’s double-digit pace confirms an economic
boom underpinning the global bull market.

### Page 5

Valuations continue to fall even as earnings soar (chart). The US MSCI’s
forward P/E is down to 18.9. Japan’s has dropped to 14.2 from 14.7 a
week ago. The All Country World ex-US trades at 12.0. The Emerging
Markets MSCI has slipped to single digits and is at 8.6 now.
III. Bonds
Sovereign bonds continued to rally worldwide this week as investors fled
the AI trade (chart). The US 10-year Treasury yield fell to 4.86%. The UK's
yield remains the highest in the group, at 5.16%, followed by Australia’s

5.00%. France’s yield has dropped to 4.77%, back below the US yield.
Japan’s yield is down to 2.63%.

### Page 6

IV. Focus on South Korea & Taiwan
South Korea's forward earnings is still rising to record highs, though more
slowly than earlier in the year (chart). The stock market isn’t buying it. The
MSCI South Korea stock price index is down more than 45% from its
record high. Its forward P/E has collapsed to 3.4, the lowest in over 20
years. Investors are betting that analysts will have to slash their earnings
estimates.
Taiwan tells the same story, but with a more sensible valuation. Its forward

earnings is also at a record high. Its forward P/E has dropped to 14.4 from
23.2 earlier this year (chart).

### Page 7

Fundamental data suggest South Korea and Taiwan should continue to
benefit from US demand for technology hardware. US imports of selected
technologies rose to a record $61.3 billion in September (chart).
Asia supplies much of that hardware. US imports from Vietnam rose to
$28.7 billion in September, and imports from Taiwan rose to $28.4 billion
(chart). Imports from South Korea rose to $19.2 billion. A slower US data
center buildout would delay that demand, not derail it. We would view a

slower pace as more sustainable over a longer period of time.

### Page 8

clause.

## Document 397: Global Views_ Downside Growth Risks

Source PDF: all report pdf/Global Views_ Downside Growth Risks.pdf
Report source: Goldman Sachs Publishing
Date: 2026-10-26
Pages: 10
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

Jan Hatzius 
+1(212)902-0394 | jan.hatzius@gs.com 
Goldman Sachs & Co. LLC
Global Views: Downside Growth Risks
26 October 2026 | 6:45AM EDT
1. So far in 2026, global growth has been remarkably unremarkable.  Both the world 
as a whole and the G3 economies are growing in line with our estimates of potential 
GDP .  Our interpretation is that fiscal easing and the positive impulse from the AI 
boom have offset the negative impulse from the war in Iran and the lingering drag 
from higher US tariffs.

2. Our standing global GDP forecasts project more of the same in 2027, as lower 
energy prices offset fading tailwinds from fiscal policy and AI.  But the balance of 
risks is on the downside.  The most immediate concern is that, far from subsiding, 
energy prices have again climbed sharply on fears of renewed Middle East escalation.  
The prices of gasoline, diesel, jet fuel, and European/Asian natural gas are now near 
or even above their March/April highs, with likely negative effects on consumers and 
energy-importing economies more broadly.

### Page 2

3. Although we don’t expect significant fiscal policy changes in 2027, the consequences 
of large-scale deficits—coupled with AI-linked borrowing and central bank hikes—have 
started to show up more clearly in higher longer-term interest rates.  The moves have 
been mostly orderly, but the increase since early 2026 averages a sizable 100bp across 
DM yield curves.  If sustained, we estimate that this increase will subtract a cumulative 
0.6% from GDP via weakness in housing, consumer durables, and business investment.  
(Only part of the drag is incorporated in our standing forecasts because we expect bond 
yields to pull back as central banks deliver fewer hikes than priced in markets.)

Exhibit 2: Oil Price Risks Are Skewed to the Upside

Source: Goldman Sachs Global Investment Research

Goldman Sachs Global Views

### Page 3

4. The growth impulse from the AI boom was set to peak in late 2026 or early 2027 even 
before concerns around the sustainability of the AI investment boom intensified in 
recent weeks.  Although AI investment is still rising rapidly, we expect the increase as a 
share of GDP to slow over the next year.  Alongside the fading boost from last year’s 
increase in US depreciation allowances, this will weigh on capital spending growth, 
especially in the US.  AI has also boosted US consumer spending growth via a positive 
wealth effect worth an estimated 0.4pp (or 0.25% of GDP) over the past year.  This 
impact is likely to decline over the next year, gradually if stocks perform in line with our 
strategists’ reasonably upbeat expectations but more sharply if AI-related stocks 
continue to decline.

5. Our own base case remains that AI investment will ultimately pay off in terms of higher 
productivity and economywide profits.  However, one key channel for this payoff is labor 
market disruption, which has negative near-term macroeconomic effects.  And while DM 
labor markets still look solid in aggregate, we are starting to see clearer signs that the AI 
impact is spreading beyond the technology sector.  Across nonmanufacturing industries, 
the share of US firms that have adopted AI in regular business functions now shows a 
statistically significant negative correlation with employment growth and a statistically 
significant positive correlation with unemployment.

6. The Fed’s recent signals—explicit from Williams/Jefferson and implicit from Warsh—
effectively rule out a hike on October 28.  But the FOMC still looks very likely to deliver 
another 25bp hike to 4-4.25% on December 9 because spot inflation remains too high.  
And while we continue to expect significant disinflation next year on the back of 
reduced impulses from tariffs, energy prices, and AI-related prices, the risks to our 
forecast are on the upside, most obviously in energy.  Higher inflation could result in

7. Like the Fed, the ECB will likely hold rates steady next week and deliver a final hike (to 
2.75%) at the December meeting.  However, the turbulence in the French bond market 
poses somewhat greater downside risks to that call than in the US.  The turbulence has 
tightened financial conditions to a degree that is likely to shave ¼-½pp from annualized 
growth in coming quarters.  And if it intensifies into December, markets might perceive a 
hike as signaling insufficient focus by the ECB on the risk of Euro area fragmentation.  
While the ECB could address this issue by coupling a hike with renewed balance sheet 
expansion, the hurdle for such a move is high, in part because purchases of French 
bonds would inevitably raise concerns about moral hazard with respect to the needed 
fiscal adjustment.

November 5.  Consistent with our view that the UK economy is stronger than widely 
perceived, GDP growth has surprised on the upside this year and the unemployment rate 
declined to 4.7% in August on a 3-month average basis (with the volatile 1-month rate 
plunging to 4.1%).  Rising inflation and the looming household energy price reset in 
January imply a significant risk of further moves, although market pricing of 4+ hikes 
(including November) still looks excessive.  We also expect hikes in Canada and New 
Zealand this week and in Australia next week.

9. Slightly faster than expected growth in Q3 has pushed up our 2026 GDP forecast for 
China to 4.6%.  But most activity indicators are much weaker.  Both property activity and 
fixed asset investment are contracting year-on-year, and while nominal retail sales 
growth picked up to +1.1% in September, large retailers (whose sales are relatively 
better measured) are still posting substantial year-on-year declines.  With domestic 
demand rising slowly at best, sustained GDP growth in the official 4.5-5% target range 
will require ongoing steep increases in the trade surplus.  But the surplus is already at 1% 
of global GDP , and further increases will eat more noticeably into output and 
employment abroad.  Ultimately, China’s trading partners may respond by imposing 
trade barriers that weigh on Chinese exports and GDP , although the measures taken so 
far have been limited.

Exhibit 8: UK Growth Forecasts Have Drifted Higher

Source: Bloomberg, Goldman Sachs Global Investment Research
26 October 2026   6
Goldman Sachs Global Views

### Page 7

10. Our strongest global view is that central banks will deliver fewer hikes than currently 
priced.  In addition, our rates strategists flag underweight positioning, diminished 
technical risks, and risk asset pressures as reasons why the recent rally might extend.  
The market’s narrow breadth makes our equity strategists wary of momentum factor 
exposure, but they still expect the S&P 500 to resume its climb after the midterm 
election, as earnings continue to grow solidly.  Our credit strategists remain cautious on 
IG technology bonds but see scope for some near-term reversal in HY given the scale of 
the recent selloff.  Our FX strategists expect the Japanese yen to act as a hedge against 
further AI jitters, in part because such jitters would shrink US-Japan interest rate 
differentials.  And our commodity strategists recommend targeted exposures to oil 
products and European natural gas to hedge near-term escalation risks alongside 
longer-term exposures to copper, gold, and select power markets to benefit from 
structural supply/demand imbalances. 
Jan Hatzius

Editorial 
Increasing signs of a bottom 
Strategy summary: Democrats’ thumping win in the US mid -
term elections has three key implications: i) policy gridlock is 
back in Washington, likely stalling fiscal expansion and new 
tariffs; ii) the chance of a prolonged Middle East conflict is 
curtailed, lowering oil prices and inflation expectations; and  iii) 
the pace of AI data-centre buildout is at risk , curbing AI-linked 
bond supply. Higher corporate and wealth taxes are a long-term 
risk if Democrats win the 2028 presidential elections.   
For portfolios, the election reinforce s our mid-cycle playbook 
already in place:  remaining broadly balanced across assets, 
while looking for tactical opportunities. As the S&P500 volatility 
index (VIX) surpasses 25, we see rising signs of equity market 
bottoming. We add an opportunistic idea on the US IT sector 
(see page 4), while using elevated volatility to earn income 
through structured strategies. In bonds, we prefer the 3-7-year 
part of the Developed Market bond yield curve and BBB-to-BB-
rated corporate bonds to earn attractive income as AI supply 
eases. In FX, we position for a modestly weaker USD over 6 -
12 months by allocating to ex-US assets.  
Policy implications:  Oil fell 12% over the past week as 
Democrats captured the US Senate and House. The party also 
flipped governorships in five states – Georgia, Iowa, Nevada, 
Ohio and Texas – securing a majority of governorships among 
50 US states for the first time since 2010. Vice President Vance 
and Republican leaders attributed the defeat to the Iran conflict, 
making it harder for President Trump to sustain it. Democrat 
control of the defence budget from next year further constrains 
conflict escalation. Despite near-term escalation risks, we see 
the geopolitical risk premium in oil prices fading, with oil easing 
towards USD 80/bbl  over 6-12 months. Oil-driven disinflation 
should allow central banks to adopt less aggressive policies. 
Adding tactical exposure to US IT sector : Semiconductors 
slumped this week amid concerns over data-centre moratoria, 
while beneficiaries of Democrat policies include defensives, 
affordability-related stocks, tariff rollback beneficiaries and  the 
healthcare sector. We use the volatility to add tactical exposure 
to the US IT sector . As we enter mid -cycle dynamics, equities 
should return 8-10% annually. The 2015–2017 mid-cycle offers 
a useful comparison: with an opposition Congress,  S&P 500 
returns slowed to 8.3% , but remained positive. Diversification 
across sectors and regions remains a priority amid the rotation. 
Prefer belly of the bond curve: Government bonds rallied on 
expectations of slower growth. We still expect two more Fed 
hikes by H1 2027, given sticky near-term inflation and a robust 
economy. However, cheaper oil should lower inflation towards 
the Fed’s 2% target next year, allowing rate cuts in H2 2027. 
Ultra-long yields remain elevated due to  fiscal challenges. 
Bonds, particularly those from non-hyperscaler investment -
grade corporates , offer attractive income. The 3 -7-year 
segment – the belly of the yield curve – offers the best balance 
of income and sensitivity to interest-rate changes. 
Credit to benefit from restrained AI supply:  Since states  
control data centre regulations, Democrat wins in governorship 
races in leading data centre states, such as Texas and Georgia, 
means growth in this segment is likely to be restricted. Slower 
data-centre construction could improve credit supply -demand 
conditions due to reduced issuance by hyperscalers. However, 
some data -centre-focused deals could become less viable, 
warranting caution towards lower -quality issuers. High -yield 
credit spreads have widened, but BB- and B-rated bonds have 
held up, while CCC -rated debt has r eached distressed levels. 
We prefer the BBB–BB range, spanning the boundary between 
investment-grade and high-yield debt, for income. 
A modest headwind for the USD, tailwind for gold: The 
dollar should remain range-bound near term before weakening 
modestly over 6-12 months. Democrat control of the Congress 
limits tariff escalation , fiscal expansion and rapid AI buildout , 
three pillars of US exceptionalism supporting the USD through 
2026. Disinflation should also encourage a less aggressive Fed 
stance, removing another source of USD support. Near -term 
support for the USD index (DXY) remains at 100.7 . A weaker-
than-expected payrolls report tonight could accelerate the 
USD’s decline, and vice versa . A softer dollar and sustained 
central-bank buying, led by China, remain supportive of gold.  
—  Rajat Bhattacharya

Standard Chartered Bank

Top client questions 
  What are the equity market implications of the US mid -
term election results?   
Our view: The US mid -term election outcome adds political 
headwinds but does not alter our outlook for positive equity 
returns. Investors should maintain a Core allocation to global 
equities and continue to diversify their equity exposure across 
regions and sectors. We initiate an opportunistic idea on the US 
information technology (IT) sector, moving our opportunistic 
idea positioning from a de fensive tilt to a more balanced 
positioning. Higher volatility also presents opportunities to use 
structured strategies for equity exposure.  
Rationale: The Democrats’ victory in both chambers of Congress 
has supported the outperformance of themes aligned with their 
policy priorities , with clean energy stocks , including solar 
companies, among the notable beneficiaries. Affordability-related 
themes have also benefited, including companies that could gain 
from potential tariff rollbacks. Medicaid-related stocks  have also 
risen on hopes that healthcare will gain from policy changes. 
Meanwhile, semiconductor stocks remain under pressure  amid 
concerns about potential moratoriums on data centres. Our AI capital 
expenditure (capex) estimates remain conservative relative to 
consensus, but with a potential reset in expectations around data 
centre spending, investors should remain selective.  
We initiate an opportunistic idea on the US IT sector after a 
significant 17% correction this quarter. Historically, this has been 
followed by strong positive returns over the next few quarters ( see 
table). Semiconductors, about 40% of US IT, appears oversold after 
correcting more than 30% since we closed our opportunistic idea a 
month ago. Earnings growth for US IT continues to be solid, with 
volume driven growth in semiconductors, AI monetisation 
opportunities, ongoing cloud growth and resilient services revenue.  
As we highlighted in our November 2026 Global Market Outlook , 
the transition to mid -cycle is typically characterised by greater 
political headwinds. The mid -term election outcome reinforces this 
trend. The mid-cycle experience of 2015-17 offers a useful historical 
parallel for returns that moderate, but remain positive. Although we 
trimmed global equities to a Core allocation last week, we continue 
to expect equities to deliver mid-to-high single-digit returns per 
annum during the mid -cycle, even amid political headwinds, 
supported by steady earnings growth in both 2027 and 2028. 
Stay invested and diversify, with Overweight allocations to the US 
and A xJ, which offer the strongest earnings growth. Sector 
diversification is key as we expect market leadership to broaden. We 
are Overweight the US IT, US healthcare, US financial, US materials, 
Europe ex-UK financial and Japan financial sectors. 
—  Fook Hien Yap, Senior Investment Strategist

Policy uncertainty and a change in the innovation 
cycle is consistent with our view of a mid-cycle 
transition, where equity returns moderate but 
remain positive

Standard Chartered Bank 
WS Global CIO Office | 6 November 2026

Top client questions (cont’d) 
  What are the key takeaways from Q3 2026 US earnings? 
Our view:  Q3 US earnings remain robust, but more modest 
earnings beats reinforce our view that equity markets are 
entering a mid-cycle phase. We remain Overweight US equities, 
but recommend investors to maintain diversified sector 
exposure as returns moderate and market leadership broadens. 
Rationale: As of 30 October, 82% of S&P500 companies that ha d 
reported had beaten earnings estimates. However, aggregate 
earnings were just 4.5% above expectations, a smaller beat than in 
recent quarters. Still, Q3 US earnings growth remain s robust at 
31.6% y/y, supporting expected full-year growth of 36.1% in 2026 
and 16.4% in 2027 for the S&P500. The slower pace of upgrades 
suggests the bull market is maturing  as investors focus on the 
sustainability of earnings growth. This raises the hurdle for positive 
share price reactions and makes selectivity more essential. For Big 
Tech, capex commitments remain high, while free cash flow is under 
pressure and expected to remain negative. Financing risks weigh on 
sentiment, particularly for companies with weaker balance sheets.  
We remain Overweight US equities , given the strong earnings 
growth, but we expect elevated volatility  and modest returns. We 
prefer quality exposure within technology  while maintaining  
diversified exposure across US financials, healthcare and materials. 
—  Ryan Goh, Investment Strategist 
  What to expect from Japanese banks’ upcoming results?   
Our view:  We expect  Japanese banks’  earnings growth  to 
continue over the medium term, supported by higher domestic 
net interest income (NII) and resilient fee income. We remain 
positive on the sector, favouring banks with strong deposit 
franchises and scope to increase shareholder returns. 
Investors will focus on whether full -year guidance upgrades 
and buybacks exceed expectations. 
Rationale: The BoJ’s September rate hike to 1.25% took effect late 
in the quarter, so its benefit should be more visible from the 
December quarter as loans reprice. We expect three further BoJ 
rate hikes by June 2027, supporting further NII expansion.  We 
will also look for signs that stronger corporate investment is driving 
faster loan growth and higher financing and advisory fees.  
We prefer to place more weight on guidance upgrades driven by 
recurring lending and fee income than on those driven by gains on 
equity disposals. Continued reductions in cross -shareholdings 
could free up capital for additional buybacks, providing a further 
boost to return on equity  (ROE). Key risks:  faster-than-expected 
deposit rate repricing, rising credit costs and bond portfolio losses. 
—  Robin Xie, CFA, Senior Investment Strategist

Top client questions (cont’d) 
  What are the fundamental reasons behind your India 
equity upgrade? Which sectors offer favourable risk-reward? 
Our View: Indian equities offer an increasingly attractive risk -
reward profile. With India’s valuation premium to Emerging 
Markets (EMs) narrowing, foreign ownership near decade lows, 
Underweight EM positioning  and a stabilising Indian rupee 
(INR), conditions are increasingly supportive of Indian equity 
outperformance vs. the broader AxJ region.  
Rationale: We have upgraded Indian equities to Overweight within 
our AxJ equities allocation. While Indian markets  have witnessed 
USD 32bn of foreign investor outflows in 2026 year-to-date, strong 
domestic institutional investor inflows of USD 74bn  have more 
than offset foreign selling. These outflows and the Indian equity 
market’s underperformance vs . global peers reflect broader EM 
allocation shifts towards AI , rather than weakening India n 
fundamentals. Capital has gravitated toward s markets such as the 
US, Taiwan and  South Korea, which offer direct exposure to AI 
beneficiaries. However, we expect market leadership to broaden, as 
the rapid AI buildout is increasing ly debt-funded, and US mid-term 
election results raise uncertainty. We prefer to diversify and we see 
investors reallocating towards structurally strong  growth markets 
such as India, which offer non-AI driven growth.  
The RBI’s measures have supported INR stability,  but underlying 
USD demand remains elevated. Lower oil prices would improve 
external balances, but the INR is likely to follow a path of gradual, 
controlled depreciation rather than a quick downward move. On 
valuation to its own historical average, the Nifty 50 one-year forward 
P/E is trading 18% below its long-term average while ROE at 14.7% 
is above historical averages. With India now marginally Underweight 
in large EM portfolios compared to  the c.2% average Overweight 
historically, the case for a recovery in foreign flows is 
strengthening. 
India’s earnings growth remains intact . The Nifty 50 clocked Q1 
FY27 and Q2 FY27 profit-after-tax growth of c.20% y/y (ex -oil 
marketing companies) . Large -caps offer greater earnings visibility 
and downside protection, while stronger growth in small - and mid-
caps warrants a selective approach given elevated valuations.  
India’s growth outlook remains supported by a strengthening 
investment cycle, manufacturing momentum, supply chain 
diversification, resilient consumption and healthy corporate and 
banking balance sheets. Importantly, earnings growth remains 
robust and broa d-based across the financial, metals, telecom, 
automobiles and other domestic sectors, with Nifty 50 earnings 
growth expectations of c.14-15% over 2027 vis a vis A xJ’s 
technology driven earnings growth of 29% in 2027. 
—  Shaily Gang, Chief Investment Strategist, India

### Page 7

Standard Chartered Bank 
WS Global CIO Office | 6 November 2026

Top client questions (cont’d) 
  Does the emerging US job market data alter your view of 
the Fed’s rate path and government bond yields? 
Our view: We do not expect the recent and incoming US job 
market data to alter our outlook unless there are significant 
deviations from the US economy’s current trajectory.  
Rationale: The US economy continues to exhibit resilience, with the 
latest Job Openings and Labour Turnover Survey data showing job 
openings surprising to the upside at 7.5mn. Initial jobless claims 
remain near three-month lows. There is little evidence of a material 
slowdown in labour demand. Inflation is expected to trend lower 
through H2 2027, but the disinflationary impulse remains a medium-
term story and is unlikely to manifest meaningfully in the near term. 
The market is now pricing in two additional 25bps hikes by H1 
2027 (down from three previously), in line with our view. These two 
rate hikes are largely priced into the US government bond yield 
curve. We estimate 2- and 10-year US government bond yields to 
range between 4.5-4.75% and 5-5.25%, respectively, over the next 
three months . Our twelve -month forecast for 2- and 10 -year US 
government bond yields are 4.25-4.5% and 4.75-5%, respectively, 
The 2-year yield is already trading within our expected range and 
would only move materially higher if a significantly stronger -than-
expected payrolls print led markets to price in a third hike. With two 
hikes baked in, long -run disinflation expectations intact and an 
uncertain fiscal outlook, the longer end of the  yield curve is 
expected to be less sensitive to a single payrolls release. 
—  Ray Heung, Senior Investment Strategist 
  What is your outlook for the GBP following the BoE’s rate 
hike this week? Do you see any impact on EUR/GBP? 
Our view: The BoE  rate hike supports  the GBP, but upside 
remains limited. We remain constructive on EUR/GBP, 
expecting a rebound towards 0.8450. 
Rationale: The BoE hiked by 25bps to 4% as expected . The GBP 
was little changed, despite hawkish inflation guidance. With markets 
pricing only around 50bps of additional BoE tightening over the next 
12 months, we see the GBP remaining vulnerable. In contrast, the 
ECB held its deposit rate at 2.50% in October but signalled readiness 
to tighten if inflation remains elevated. We expect Euro area inflation 
to rise further, keeping the ECB’s tightening bias alive. This suggests 
EUR/GBP has fallen too far relative to interest rate differentials.  
Germany’s planned increase in federal debt issuance  should 
support Euro area growth, while Moody’s decision not to downgrade 
France has removed an important EUR tail risk. Meanwhile, tighter 
UK fiscal policy is likely to weigh on domestic growth and business 
investment. We expect EUR/GBP to recover towards 0.8450. 
—  Vincent Tan, Senior Investment Strategist 
—  Iris Yuen, Investment Strategist

We expect 2- and 10-year US government bond 
yields to range between 4.5-4.75% and 5-5.25%, 
respectively, over the next three months

### Page 8

Standard Chartered Bank 
WS Global CIO Office | 6 November 2026

Top client questions (cont’d) 
  Why is the AUD underperforming despite the RBA hike? 
Our view: The AUD has lagged because markets had largely 
priced in the RBA’s November rate hike to 4.85%, and the bank 
gave investors less reason to expect another. Equity weakness 
also weighed on the currency. We remain bullish on AUD/USD  
and would add on pullbacks. Australia’s interest rate advantage 
and a sustained recovery in risk sentiment should support a 
move towards 0.72 in three months and 0.74 in 12 months. 
Rationale: Ahead of the 3 November RBA rate decision, market 
pricing implied an 88% chance of a 25bp s hike, leaving little scope 
for an upside surprise. What changed after the announcement 
was the pricing of the next hike.  The RBA dropped its reference 
to further rate increases, and three members preferred a rate hold. 
Markets scaled back the odds of rates reaching 5.10% next year to 
around 30%, thus weighing on the AUD. 
The AUD is sensitive to swings in global risk appetite, especially 
the US tech sell-off spread to global equities, triggering margin calls 
and weighing on  the currency. An equity recovery would ease that 
pressure. Even after the two 25bp s Fed hikes we expect by mid -
2027, Australia would retain a 35bps rate advantage  over the top 
of the Fed’s target range, supporting a gradual AUD recovery. 
—  Vincent Tan, Senior Investment Strategist 
—  Iris Yuen, Investment Strategist

We see AUD/USD upside risks on a sustained 
recovery in risk sentiment

## Document 433: OpenAI推出ChatGPT企业功能，向谷歌广告市场发起挑战

Source PDF: all report pdf/OpenAI推出ChatGPT企业功能，向谷歌广告市场发起挑战.pdf
Report source: Unknown
Date: 未知日期
Pages: 3
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

OpenAI 推出 "Businesses in ChatGPT" 测试版，允许企业认领商业主⻚、管理信息并追踪
⽤⼾互动数据，核⼼⼯具包括 "Business Hub" （信息管理与数据分析）和 "Business
Agents" （ AI 客服交互），功能设计⾼度对标⾕歌商家⽣态。
摘要：
OpenAI 正将 ChatGPT 打造成企业与消费者之间的新型连接平台，直接挑战⾕歌在商业信息检索
领域的主导地位。
周⼆，OpenAI 宣布推出 "Businesses in ChatGPT" 测试版，允许企业认领并管理其在
ChatGPT 上的商业主⻚，追踪⽤⼾互动数据，并通过专属 AI 代理与潜在客⼾直接对话。
此次推出的产品体系包括两个核⼼⼯具：供企业管理信息和查看数据分析的"Business Hub"，
以及允许⽤⼾与企业 AI 客服直接交互的"Business Agents"。
后者⽬前仅向美国境内符合条件的电商及在线服务企业开放。企业还可以为其 Business Agent
投放赞助⼴告，作为 ChatGPT ⼴告的落地⽬标⻚⾯，赞助内容将予以明确标注。

功能设计对标⾕歌商业⽣态
Business Hub 的核⼼功能与⾕歌商家资料⾼度相似。
企业完成验证后，可更新关键信息、上传包含定价与库存的产品⽬录，并获取包括提及次数、
主⻚浏览量、⽹站点击量及热⻔发现主题在内的聚合分析数据。这⼀设计令外界将其与⾕歌分
析⼯具相提并论。
在接⼊路径上， OpenAI 为不同企业类型提供了差异化⽅案：
现有 OpenAI 企业客⼾及⼴告主可通过专属客⼾经理操作；
Shopify 商家则可直接从 Business Hub 或 Shopify 后台完成店铺绑定，其符合条件的商品已通
过 Shopify Catalog 整合⾄ ChatGPT 。
⾸⻚ 资讯 快讯 ⾏情 ⽇历 APP VIP 会员 ⼤师课 ⻅闻历 搜索

### Page 2

在数据隐私⽅⾯， OpenAI 表⽰不会向企业共享⽤⼾的具体对话内容或 Business Agent 聊天记
录，所有分析数据均经过聚合处理，且仅在互动量达到⼀定阈值后⽅可查看。
AI 代理重塑⽤⼾购买决策路径
Business Agents 的推出代表着 ChatGPT 在商业场景中的能⼒跃升。
当某企业出现在模型回复中时，⽤⼾可点击进⼊该企业的 ChatGPT 主⻚，并选择与其 Business
Agent 展开对话，就产品差异、功能匹配等具体问题进⾏深度咨询。
这⼀交互模式旨在填补消费者在购买决策前的信息获取缺⼝。以⽤⼾⽐较两款商品为例，
Business Agent 可基于企业⾃有信息提供个性化的功能对⽐与建议。
OpenAI 强调， Business Agent 对话会被清晰标注，与 ChatGPT 原有对话界⾯严格分离，以区别
于 ChatGPT 的独⽴回答。
OpenAI 副总裁 Benji Shomair 表⽰，企业验证将采⽤多种⽅式，针对不同市场和不同类型的企业
有所差异，但未披露具体流程细节。

电商直购尝试折戟后的战略转向
此次推出商业主⻚功能，是 OpenAI 在电商领域的⼀次重要战略调整。
此前，该公司曾试图将 ChatGPT 打造成可直接购物的电商平台，甚⾄引⼊了购物⻋功能，并采
⽤佣⾦分成模式，⼀度被视为与亚⻢逊正⾯竞争的重要筹码。然⽽，据报道该项⽬未能实现预

### Page 1

THE RED

WESTPAC ECONOMICS
3
OVERVIEW
Coming into the home strait for 2026,
the Australian consumer has again
got the staggers. Spending has held
up surprisingly well given the intense
headwinds that have buffeted household
budgets over the year. But the same cannot
be said for sentiment which has fallen off
badly in the face of more shocks.

Our October Red Book surveys the scene.
Psychologically, consumers has seen a
return to the ‘cost of living nightmare’
they struggled with through much of
2022, 2023 and 2024. Global forces
and domestic factors are again having
pervasive negative impacts on budgets.
The scale may not be as severe as the
previous episode – inflation is running in
the 3-4% range not the 7-8% and interest
rates have risen 1ppt not 4 – but the
feeling is similar. In part this because there

has was very little reprieve in between –
despite tax cuts and a partial unwind of the
interest rate tightening in 2025, household
incomes never got a chance to regain the
lost ground.
The inflation ‘relapse’, and the difficulty
the RBA and governments seem to be
having in managing the situation, is likely
feeding into fears about the outlook for the
wider economy as well.
Exactly what this means for spending is
not entirely clear. As discussed in our last

report , sentiment reads have been much
weaker than actual spending in recent
years. The apparent disconnect is likely
due to a mix of legacy effects from COVID,
and the specific challenges posed by high
inflation (including the lingering effects
of high prices even once inflation has
subsided).
On balance we expect the spending side
to slow materially, particularly as we
head into year end. Indeed, the seven
weeks between now and Christmas
could prove decisive for the consumer

outlook, beginning with another important
sentiment survey update and then the high
season for retail. How spending performs
outside of key sales windows will be most
telling.

1. Consumer spending: slowdown ahead
“... sentiment ... has fallen off badly in the face of

### Page 4

WESTPAC ECONOMICS
4
The Westpac–Melbourne Institute
Consumer Sentiment Index declined 4.2%
over the three months to Oct, unwinding a
mini-rally through the middle of the year.
At 80.4 the average read over the Oct
survey implies that pessimists outnumber
optimists by nearly 20%, a deeply
pessimistic level by historical standards

that is amongst the forty worst reads since
the survey began in the 1973.
The survey detail shows concerns are still
primarily about the cost-of-living and high
interest rates than the more acute job and
insolvency problems that arise during a
recession. The RBA’s Sep rate hike, mid-
way through the survey week looks to have
badly rattled consumers.
The component detail shows a broad
pull-back but with bigger declines in
assessments of family finances and

buyer sentiment and milder declines in
assessments of the economic outlook.
Consumers remain intensely risk averse.
The Westpac Risk Aversion Index held near
historical highs in Sep, readings still only a
touch of the 52-year high of 60.8 recorded
in June 2023. Recall that the index is based
on responses to questions on the ‘wisest
place for savings’. Responses show ‘safe
options’ remain very heavily favoured.
The sentiment mix points to a significant

weakening in spending momentum is
weakening. CSI±, a modified indicator
that shows a better correlation with per
capita spending, points to a shift back to
significant outright contractions in per
capita spending. That said, spending
in 2026 has held up better than these
indicators have suggested so far.
Consumers are on high alert for interest
rate. The Westpac–Melbourne Institute
Mortgage Rate Expectations Index rose
5.5% to 179 over the three months to

Oct, just shy of May’s cycle peak of 181.
The RBA has raised interest rates 25bps
since then. The Nov survey update due Nov
10 will be an important update on how
expectations have evolved.
The ‘time to buy a major item’ index
dropped 4.3% over the last three months,
taking it back near the lows seen earlier
in the year and a full 40pts below the
long run avg. Durables spend has been
mixed over the first half of 2026, posting a
surprisingly solid gain in Q2 but with most

of this centred on new vehicle spend where
‘extenuating circumstances’ were at play.
Homebuyer sentiment has been
understandably unsettled in recent months
given heightened uncertainty around
interest rate and tax policy changes amid
evidence of a broadening downturn in
established markets.
The ‘time to buy a dwelling’ index actually
lifted 3.6% over the three months to Oct
but is still at firmly pessimistic levels. Both
monthly reads and sub-group detail have

been very unsettled.
The broadening and deepening downturn
in Australia’s housing markets is seeing
an equally rapid shift in consumer house
price expectations although these have not
turned as negative as they did during the
2022-23 correction.
The Westpac–Melbourne Institute
Consumer House Price Expectations Index
declined a further 2.4% over the 3mths to
Oct, following a steep 25% drop over the
previous 3mths. At 115.6, the Oct index
read is miles below the 15yr high of 174 at

the start of the year and below the long run
avg of 130. That said, the read is still above
100, meaning more consumers expect
prices to rise than fall.
The labour market remains an island of
relative calm for consumers. The Westpac–
Melbourne Institute Unemployment
Expectations Index rose 9.3% over the
three months to Oct, reversing most of
the 12.1% improvement over the previous
three months (recall that higher reads mean
more consumers expect unemployment to

rise over the year ahead). At 142.2, the Oct
index read is now decisively above the long
run avg of 129 again but  still well below
the levels that would sound more worrying
alarms.
As noted, the Nov survey, currently in the
field and due to be released on Nov 10,
will provide critical updates on many of
these themes.

WESTPAC ECONOMICS
5
THE CONSUMER MOOD
• Consumers remain stuck in a cost-of-
living nightmare that seems to have
not end in sight. Sentiment remains
deeply pessimistic, weighed down by
continued uncertainty about the path
for inflation and interest rates, the
latter providing a clear negative ‘jolt’
midway through the Oct survey week.

• The Westpac Melbourne Institute
Index of Consumer Sentiment declined
4.2% over the three months to Oct,
unwinding a mini-rally through the
middle of the year. At 80.4 the average
read over the Oct survey implies that
pessimists outnumber optimists by
nearly 20%, a deeply pessimistic level
by historical standards. It is amongst
the forty worst reads since the survey
began in the 1973. We have had
two others in this unfortunate group
already this year (in Apr and Jun) with

another eight in 2022–23. Altogether
this marks the worst period of recurring
extremely weak sentiment since the
disastrous recession in the early 1990s.
• The survey detail shows consumer
concerns are still primarily about the
cost-of-living and high interest rates
than the more acute job and insolvency
problems that arise during a recession.
• Average weekly pump prices pushed
back over $2.30/litre nationally in early
Oct, rising to over $2.35/litre since. This

is near the peaks seen back in April and
up nearly 25% since the start of the
year. Meanwhile the RBA interest rate
rise in late Sep took the cash rate to
4.6%, the highest since 2011, with the
standard variable mortgage interest
rate pushing above 9% for the first time
since 2008.
• The RBA’s Sep move badly rattled
consumers. Sentiment amongst the
60% of the sample surveyed prior came
in at 86.9, up slightly from the Sep
read. The sentiment read across the

40% surveyed after the RBA decision
came in at just 67.2, an alarmingly weak
read that, for complete surveys, has
only been registered during the depths
of the early 1990s recession. The nearly
20% drop between pre and post RBA
samples is also the largest negative
swing we have seen since we started
tracking daily responses in 2019.
• The RBA has delivered another hike as
we go to press, meaning Nov is likely
to see yet another interest rate hike

to sentiment (the survey result will be
released on Nov 10).
